Poland Final Tables (1st and 2nd level)

In all the tables below:
teams marked with 'p' are promoted to higher division or to play-offs
teams marked with 'r' are relegated to lower  division
teams marked with '>' play in relegation playoffs
teams marked with '<' play in promotion playoffs
team  marked with 'c' is the champion
teams marked with 'u' hold next season UEFA Cup places
team  marked with 'i' holds next season Intertoto place

Below the first division (or championship) tables goalkeepers, field
players and coaches of the winning teams are presented, with the
number of matches, minutes on field, goals scored, as +x (or admitted in
the case of goalkeepers, as -x), and date of birth. The # symbol means
that the player started his career in that club. Sendings off during
season are marked by asterisks. See also 1910-1920. 

For easier orientation in the huge file, I've added a table of links:
  • Early championship finals: 1920 (abandoned) | 1921 | 1922 | 1923 | 1924 (not held) | 1925 | 1926
  • Pre-war league: 1927 | 1928 | 1929 | 1930 | 1931 | 1932 | 1933 | 1934 | 1935 | 1936 | 1937 | 1938 | 1939 (unfinished)
  • Post-war championship finals: 1946 | 1947
  • Spring-autumn league: 1948 | 1949 | 1950 | 1951 | 1952 | 1953 | 1954 | 1955 | 1956 | 1957 | 1958 | 1959 | 1960 | 1961 | 1962 (spring)
  • Autumn-spring league (14 teams): 1962/63 | 1963/64 | 1964/65 | 1965/66 | 1966/67 | 1967/68 | 1968/69 | 1969/70 | 1970/71 | 1971/72 | 1972/73
  • Autumn-spring league (16 teams): 1973/74 | 1974/75 | 1975/76 | 1976/77 | 1977/78 | 1978/79 | 1979/80 | 1980/81 | 1981/82 | 1982/83 | 1983/84 | 1984/85 | 1985/86 | 1986/87 | 1987/88 | 1988/89 | 1989/90 | 1990/91
  • Autumn-spring league (18 teams): 1991/92 | 1992/93 | 1993/94 | 1994/95 | 1995/96 | 1996/97 | 1997/98
  • Autumn-spring league (16 teams): 1998/99 | 1999/00 2000/01 




From 1921 to 1926 the championship was decided in a series of tournaments
played between five (1921), eight (1922-23) and nine (1925-26) teams.
The 1920 championship was abandoned due to the Soviet war. The 1924
championship didn't take place because of Olympic Games preparations.


Poland 1920

Abandoned due to the Soviet War. Winners of five regional
groups were to meet in the final tournament.

Lvovian Group

Only two matches played: Pogoń Lwów - Czarni Lwów 3:1 and Czarni
Lwów - Polonia Przemyśl 6:0. Rewera Stanisławów played no match.

Lodzian Group

No matches played. Teams entered: ŁKS, Klub Turystów, ŁTSG and (ŁTG)
Szturm, all from Łódź.

Poland 1927

From 1927 to 1939 there existed a formal league, first composed only from
the teams affiliating to Polish Football Association (PZPN), joined in 1928
by Cracovia Kraków. There was no second division; instead, winners
of the regional leagues (usually four, called "B-classes") would met in a
tournament ("A-class") to decide the one (two from 1936) promoted team(s).

incorporated into Chorzów in 1939. Hence the uniform appellation.

Poland 1933

Due to planned reorganisation the championship was fought out in two
stages: first in two groups of 6 teams, then for the title in a group
of three best teams from each qualification group. The rest of the teams
played for the relegation; the two last teams played with the second best
A-class team; the winner stayed in the league.

No promotion? (some (?) 3rd division groups played, as it seems, in three
rounds: autumn of 1952 and the whole of 1953, with teams relegated in
1952; there were thus separate tables to determine promotion and relegation
etc.) Playoffs were ordered between some teams and 3rd level group winners,
but these remained irrelevant as the 2nd level was cut down to 14 teams.

All the clubs connected with the Polish Army, except Legia, were removed from
further competition. The list includes OWKS Wawel Kraków (runners up), Lotnik
Warszawa (5th in 2nd division) and OWKS Zawisza Bydgoszcz (8th in 2nd
division).
No teams were allowed to take their places, so for the next season
both 1st and 2nd divisions have 11 teams competing.

> 16 MZKS Mikulczyce            30  23   7  9 14  21 33  0:3 1:1 0:1 1:1 1:1 0:2 1:2 2:1 1:3 1:0 0:1 1:2 0:1 1:0 1:0  x

The second division was extended to 32 teams. This entrained a complicated promotion system

Promoted (direct): STAL Stalowa Wola, METAL Kluczbork, STOCZNIOWIEC Gdańsk,
LUBLINIANKA Lublin and the teams that finished on the 2nd and 3rd places
in the so-called "M" league (third division)

Southwestern Group: BKS Bielsko, GKS Jastrzębie
Southern Group: GARBARNIA Kraków, GKS Tychy
Eastern Group: LUBLINIANKA Lublin, AVIA Świdnik
Northern Group: BAŁTYK Gdynia, WARTA Poznań

The teams to be relegated (places 13-16) played with teams that finished
on the 4th places in the "M" league:

URSUS Warszawa - MGKS Mikulczyce 0:0, 3:0 (MGKS was renamed to Sparta
                                           Zabrze during those matches)
ARKONIA Szczecin - Górnik Wałbrzych 1:0, 2:0
URANIA Ruda Śląska - Moto Jelcz Oława 1:0, 0:1. 4:3 on pen.
AKS Niwka - Górnik Wojkowice 2:1, 5:1

And the loser teams played for another two places:

SPARTA Zabrze - Górnik Wałbrzych 3:0, 0:1
Górnik Wojkowice - MOTO JELCZ Oława 0:0, 0:3 (? - probably 3:0, since
                                              Górnik is found in the
                                              table below!)

That gave 10+4+8+4+2 = 28 teams, a number short of 4 from the planned 32.
So the Polish FA decided on a spectacular, if bizarre, move: it co-opted
the best third-division teams from those regions that have not had
yet any team in the second league. That included STOMIL Olsztyn, WŁÓKNIARZ
Białystok and GWARDIA Koszalin. But that still left them with one lacking
team, so they pretended that Zielona Góra counts too, DESPITE Lechia's
second-division brief spell back in 1952, and ZASTAL Zielona Góra got
co-opted as well. Now that's far-fetching...

Poland 1992/93

This season's final table deserves a few lines of comment.
Legia and ŁKS were deducted 2 points due to match fixing
allegation. The controversial games were played on the last
day of the season. Legia beat Wisła in Kraków 6:0 while
LKS destroyed Olimpia at home 7:1. The results seemed to be
suspicious at the time because both teams were almost
head-to-head in the hunt for the championship. In the outcome
Polish FA (PZPN) decided to give the championship to Lech Poznań.

No Polish teams played in the UEFA Cup this year as a result of a UEFA
ban.

Values to determine the standings (from 1999/00 onwards):
- points,
- head-to-head points,
- head-to-head goal difference,

top teams. A two-legged play-off is played instead.

promoted: ARKA Gdynia, SZCZAKOWIANKA Jaworzno, HUTNIK Kraków, JAGIELLONIA Białystok

Poland 2001/02

The championship was fought out in two stages: first in two groups of 8
teams, then for the title and for avoiding relegations in groups formed
from teams 1-4 and 5-8 from each group, with half the points (rounded
down) from the first stage. As each and every of the previous "two
group" league arrangements, this one lasted for one year only as well.

play-offs: 

Górnik Łęczna - KSZO Ostrowiec 0:1 
KSZO Ostrowiec - Górnik Łęczna 2:1

Szczakowianka Jaworzno - RKS Radomsko 2:0
RKS Radomsko - SZCZAKOWIANKA Jaworzno 1:0
SZCZAKOWIANKA Jaworzno - RKS Radomsko 3:0 wo. (to be played in Płock)

After losing the playoffs on the field, Radomsko protested that 
Szczakowianka fielded an ineligible player (Branko Rasic JUG) in 
several second division matches. Awarding walkovers against Szczakowianka
in those matches would have resulted in a table reshuffle, making 
GKS Bełchatów finish on a playoff spot. While PZPN did not award the
wo-s, it suggested that a third match be played on neutral ground to
settle all controversy - which Radomsko refused to attend, forfeiting
their league place.
